Let me think about this task. We need to prove strong normalization for STLC with CBV semantics and if-then-else. Terms are locally nameless with free variables (atoms) and the typing judgment uses open with fvar.

The standard approach: logical relation / reducibility candidates for SN. Since we need to prove strong normalization for closed well-typed terms, we need to handle open terms under binders. The typing rule for abstraction uses cofinite quantification (forall x not in L). We need to prove the fundamental theorem for arbitrary contexts Gamma, with a context interpretation for free variables.

Approach: Define a unary logical relation `LR T t` (or reducibility). For CBV, a typical logical relation:

- For Bool: SN t (strongly normalizing) — or more precisely, "t is SN" maybe with a notion of "neutral"?
- For arrow T1 -> T2: forall v, LR T1 v -> value v -> SN (app t v) (and maybe SN t too).

But there's a subtlety: the fundamental theorem gives LR T t implies SN t. For a closed term of arrow type, we want to know it's SN. In CBV, the standard reducibility candidate for arrow type: t reducible at T1->T2 iff t is SN and for all v, value v and reducible at T1 implies app t v reducible at T2. Since we need SN of the term itself, we should include SN t in the definition. Actually, for the "hereditary" approach: define CR T t:

- CR Bool t := SN t
- CR (T1 -> T2) t := SN t /\ forall v, value v -> CR T1 v -> CR T2 (tm_app t v)

Hmm, but SN t combined with app condition. Actually the app condition plus t being a value might imply SN t? Not necessarily for open terms. Better include SN.

Wait, but the fundamental theorem for T_Abs: need CR (T1->T2) (lambda T1 t). Need SN (lambda T1 t) — easy since abstraction is a value and values are SN? Actually SN (tm_abs T t) requires for all t', step (abs) t' -> SN t' — abs is a value, so no steps, so yes SN. And for any v with value v and CR T1 v, need CR T2 (app (abs) v). We need to show app (abs T t) v steps to open t v, and CR T2 (open t v). By typing, for fresh x, open t (fvar x) has type T2 in context x|->T1;Gamma. By IH, CR T2 (open t (fvar x)) under the context interpretation. Then by a substitution lemma for the logical relation: if CR T1 v and CR T2 (open t (fvar x)) where x not free in... hmm, need to swap fvar x with v and preserve reducibility. Standard "CR is closed under substitution of related terms" lemma.

But there's the issue: SN for terms with free variables. We define SN on all terms via step relation which includes free variables; free variables don't step. But CR T (fvar x) for a context interpretation: we need to assign to each free variable a value (or term) of the appropriate type that is reducible. Standard: context interpretation: forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> CR T (sigma x)? Hmm.

Alternative simpler approach: since we only need the theorem for empty context, maybe we can avoid a general context interpretation by using the substitution lemma directly and proving fundamental theorem for contexts where each free variable is mapped to a reducible term. But the typing rules are cofinite, and the typing context Gamma is arbitrary. In T_Var case, Gamma x = Some T, and we need CR T (fvar x). So context interpretation must give CR T (fvar x) for all variables in Gamma. But fvar x is not a value... SN (fvar x) holds trivially. For arrow type, CR (T1->T2) (fvar x) requires forall v, value v -> CR T1 v -> CR T2 (app (fvar x) v). Hmm, that's not provable for an uninterpreted variable.

Standard approach: interpret variables by substituting them with values. I.e., define a "closing substitution" sigma from atoms to terms, with CR at appropriate types, and prove that if Gamma |- t : T and sigma realizes Gamma then CR T (sigma t) (where sigma applied to term substitutes fvars). Then the theorem for empty context: sigma = identity (no free vars), get CR T t, hence SN t.

Actually we can avoid defining a general substitution operation on terms and instead instantiate the free variables via a parallel substitution. Let me think of the cleanest formalization path in Rocq.

Alternative trick often used in software foundations-like settings: define the logical relation on locally closed terms, and use "open with fvar" and cofinite quantification. The fundamental theorem needs to handle free variables from Gamma. We could instead prove a "semantic typing" relation for open terms with a context of values.

Actually, there's a neat trick: prove backward closure of CR using the fact that step is deterministic (functional). We can prove `step_deterministic : forall t t1 t2, t --> t1 -> t --> t2 -> t1 = t2`. Then if t --> t' and CR t', SN t follows: any reduct t'' of t equals t', so SN t''. And the application condition: for any v, app t v: need CR T2 (app t v). We know t' ∈ CR so CR T2 (app t' v). We need "app t v" reduces in one step to app t' v (ST_App1, need locally_closed v — values are locally closed), and CR is backward closed — circular. We can prove backward closure by induction on type simultaneously with SN part. For arrow: if t --> t' and CR_arrow t', then CR_arrow t: SN t via determinism and SN t'; and for v: CR T2 (app t v) — by IH (backward closure at T2) applied to step app t v --> app t' v (ST_App1 with locally_closed v) and CR T2 (app t' v). This is well-founded induction on type for backward closure. Since backward closure at T2 is used for arbitrary terms (not subterms), we need induction on type structure — T2 is a subterm of T = T1->T2, so structural induction on T works. Good.

So need: determinism of step, values locally closed, and backward closure of CR.

Now, the substitution fundamental theorem. Let's write the definitions.

```coq
Definition SN := strongly_normalizing.

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

Actually we might not need fv for the proof if we choose x fresh via a different method. We need x not in L and x not free in t1. We could define fv. It's easy.

Alternatively, since atoms are nat, define `fresh (L : list atom) : atom := 1 + fold_right max 0 L`? But also need not free in t1. We can choose x = fresh (L ++ fv t1).

Hmm, one more consideration: the substitution sigma extended — we need sigma to map x to v, and we need to prove subst sigma' t1 = subst sigma t1 given x ∉ fv t1. Easy lemma.

Now let's define CR:

```coq
Fixpoint CR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => SN t /\ forall v, value v -> CR T1 v -> CR T2 (tm_app t v)
  end.
```

Note: `CR T1 v` is defined recursively on T1, so this is a well-founded fixpoint on T. Fine as a Fixpoint returning a Prop.

Lemma: CR_sn : forall T t, CR T t -> SN t.
- Bool: trivial. Arrow: proj1.

Wait, need SN t included for arrow. Yes.

But is CR T v for values v derivable? For T_Abs case, the context realization needs CR T1 v given v value; that's a hypothesis of the app condition (we're proving CR arrow for t, and the app condition supplies v with value and CR T1). Fine.

Backward closure lemma:

```coq
Lemma CR_step_back : forall T t t', CR T t' -> t --> t' -> CR T t.
Proof.
  induction T; intros t t' HCR Hstep.
  - (* Bool *) simpl in *. apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hstep''.
    assert (t'' = t') by (eapply step_deterministic; eauto).
    subst. apply HCR.
  - (* Arrow *) simpl in *. destruct HCR as [HSN HCR2]. split.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hstep''.
      assert (t'' = t') by (eapply step_deterministic; eauto).
      subst. apply HSN.
    + intros v Hv HCRv. apply IHT2 with (t' := tm_app t' v).
      * apply HCR2; assumption.
      * apply ST_App1; [assumption | apply value_lc; assumption].
Qed.
```

But hold on: SN_intro applied to t with proof forall t'', t --> t'' -> SN t'': we have Hstep'' : t --> t'', Hstep : t --> t', determinism gives t'' = t'. Actually determinism should be `step t t'' -> step t t' -> t'' = t'`. Good.

```coq
Lemma step_deterministic : forall t t1 t2, t --> t1 -> t --> t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2.
  induction H1; intros t2 H2; inversion H2; subst; auto;
    try (exfalso; eapply value_no_step; eauto); ...
```

If we prove full-beta SN for typable (closed) terms, then CBV SN follows immediately (any infinite CBV sequence is an infinite full-beta sequence, since CBV step implies full-beta step).

  Careful: our full-beta relation should be defined as a new inductive `step_f` (or `fstep`) that contains CBV step. Then SN is defined for `-->` (CBV). Hmm, target theorem is SN w.r.t. CBV step. If we prove SN w.r.t. full-beta step (as an SN_f predicate), then we need to derive SN (CBV) from SN_f. Since CBV step ⊆ fstep, an infinite CBV sequence is infinite fstep sequence: prove `SN_f t -> strongly_normalizing t` by induction on SN_f: SN_f t = forall t', fstep t t' -> SN_f t'; need forall t', step t t' -> SN t'; step ⊆ fstep, get SN_f t', apply IH. ✓.

So the whole proof can be about the superset relation fstep, which has nicer closure properties (no lc premises, no value premises). That's a big simplification! Let's define fstep (call it `fstep` or `step_full`):

```coq
Inductive fstep : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  | F_AppAbs : forall T t u, fstep (tm_app (tm_abs T t) u) (open t u)
  | F_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, fstep t1 t1' -> fstep (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2)
  | F_App2 : forall t1 t2 t2', fstep t2 t2' -> fstep (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1 t2')
  | F_IfTrue : forall t1 t2, fstep (tm_if tm_true t1 t2) t1
  | F_IfFalse : forall t1 t2, fstep (tm_if tm_false t1 t2) t2
  | F_If : forall t1 t1' t2 t3, fstep t1 t1' -> fstep (tm_if t1 t2 t3) (tm_if t1' t2 t3).
```

Claim: step ⊆ fstep. Prove by induction on step derivation, constructor-wise. Easy.

Standard reducibility candidates require closure under reduction and "neutral" terms. This is the classic Girard proof. Let's set it up:

Definition candidate (C : tm -> Prop) :=
  (forall t t', C t -> fstep t t' -> C t') /\  (* closed under reduction *)
  (forall t, (forall t', fstep t t' -> C t') -> ... hmm *)

Actually the standard: a set C of terms is a reducibility candidate if:
(CR1) C ⊆ SN
(CR2) C is closed under reduction: t ∈ C and t --> t' implies t' ∈ C
(CR3) if t is neutral and all t' with t --> t' are in C, then t ∈ C.

In Coq, `induction H` on H : SN (app t u) where the goal is SN t... The induction principle for SN: P (SN t) := ... If we set up the proof as `intros t u H; induction H`, the motive is fun x _ => ... hmm, H's index is (app t u), and Coq will generalize the goal to `match` situation. This is the classic "induction on SN with the term being a compound expression" issue; typically you need to generalize dependent. Let me instead prove SN_app_l by well-founded/strong induction using the SN_intro structure:

Lemma SN_app1_inv: forall t u, SN (app t u) -> SN t.
Proof.
  intros t u H. induction H as [s Hreds]. (* Hreds : forall s', app t u --> s' -> SN s' *)
  (* But s is app t u; Coq's induction on H may replace occurrences of app t u by s in the goal SN t, breaking it. *)
  
Better: prove by induction on H generalized: 
  assert (H' : forall t u, SN (tm_app t u) -> SN t) by ... using `remember` or induction on the SN derivation with a custom motive:
  induction H using ... 

Alternative approach: prove SN t directly via SN_intro: 
  Lemma SN_app_l : forall t u, SN (app t u) -> SN t.
  Proof.
    intros t u H.
    induction H as [s Hs]. (* s = app t u, Hs : forall s', s --> s' -> SN s' *)
    (* Goal: SN t — but s has been substituted?   Qed.

Actually we can just use `induction H` after `remember (tm_app t u) as s eqn:Heq`? The standard pattern:

  intros t u H. remember (tm_app t u) as s eqn:Heq. revert t u Heq. induction H; intros t u Heq; subst; ...

With `revert t u`, the induction is on H with goal forall t u, app t u = s -> SN t. In the SN_intro case with s: IH: forall t' , s --> t' -> forall a b, app a b = t' -> SN a. Lemma SN_app_l : forall t u, SN (tm_app t u) -> SN t.
Proof.

  induction H as [s Hs]; intros t u Heq; subst.
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  eapply Hs. apply F_App1. exact Hstep. 
  (* Hs : forall t', (app t u) --> t' -> SN t'; we need SN t' after the step; Hs (app t' u) (F_App1 Hstep) : SN (app t' u); then need to convert to SN t' via the IH? *)

Wait but Hs is the hypothesis of SN_intro (forall s', s --> s' -> SN s'), and the IH is over the same H structure. Hmm, in Coq's induction on an inductive Prop, when the constructor is SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t, doing `induction H as [s Hs]` gives IH: forall t' (Hstep : s --> t'), P t' where P is the motive, i.e., forall a b, app a b = t' -> SN a.   apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  apply (IH (tm_app t' u)).
  - apply F_App1. exact Hstep.
  - reflexivity.

Now let's lay out the whole proof plan concretely.

Definitions:

```coq
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom := ...
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```

Substitution composition lemma: subst sigma1 (subst sigma2 t) = subst (fun x => subst sigma1 (sigma2 x)) t. Might be needed for context extension? Hmm, in the T_Abs case we use σ' = extend σ x u directly, and need subst σ' t1 = subst σ t1 when x ∉ fv t1. We can prove that directly by induction on t1 using fv spec. Also need subst σ' (fvar x) = u: by definition.

Also need subst_open: subst σ (open t u) = open (subst σ t) (subst σ u). Prove by induction on t (generalizing k). Let's verify: open_rec k u t. subst commutes structurally. Yes.

Actually careful: In the T_Abs case we need:
  subst σ' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst σ t1) u.
Proof:
  rewrite subst_open. 
  subst σ' (fvar x) = σ' x = u (definition of extend).
  subst σ' t1 = subst σ t1 (since x ∉ fv t1).
✓.

- Arrow: t ∈ CR arrow means forall u, CR T1 u -> CR T2 (app t u). Need forall u, CR T1 u -> CR T2 (app t' u). Given HCRu, have CR T2 (app t u); app t u --> app t' u via F_App1; CR_step at T2 gives CR T2 (app t' u). ✓. Uses CR_step at T2 (subterm).

Now the mutual induction: We have four properties:
P1(T) := forall t, CR T t -> SN_f t.
P2(T) := forall t t', CR T t -> fstep t t' -> CR T t'.
P3(T) := forall t, neutral t -> (forall t', fstep t t' -> CR T t') -> CR T t.
P4(T) := forall k, CR T (tm_bvar k).

But note P3 at arrow's proof needs an SN-induction whose statement uses P1(T1), P2(T1), P3(T2). P1 at arrow uses P4(T1), P1(T2), and SN_app_l. P2 at arrow uses P2(T2). P4 at arrow uses P3(T2), P1(T1), P2(T1).

The mutual induction on T: We prove forall T, P1 T /\ P2 T /\ P3 T /\ P4 T by induction T. In the arrow case, we have IHT1 : P1 T1 /\ P2 T1 /\ P3 T1 /\ P4 T1 and IHT2 similarly. So all needed component properties are available at T1 and T2. ✓.

But there's a subtlety: P3 at arrow uses an induction on SN u, and within that, the IH refers to the same P3 statement at T2 and P2 at T1 — these are fixed, fine, no circularity because we're doing induction on SN, not on T.

However, implementing the mutual induction in Coq: `induction T; split; ...` with conjunctions can get unwieldy but manageable. Alternatively, prove the four lemmas separately using `induction T` and referring to the others at subterms — impossible because they're not yet proven. So conjunction or a combined "CR_ok T" definition. Let's do a Record or nested conjunction. Maybe define:

```
Definition CR_ok (T : ty) : Prop :=
  (forall t, CR T t -> SN_f t) /\
  (forall t t', CR T t -> fstep t t' -> CR T t') /\
  (forall t, neutral t -> (forall t', fstep t t' -> CR T t') -> CR T t) /\
  (forall k, CR T (tm_bvar k)).
```

Finally, the target theorem:

```
Theorem normalization : forall t T, empty |- t : T -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  (* get CR T t with sigma := identity *)
  assert (HCR : CR T t).
  { apply (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
    intros x T' Heq. simpl in Heq. (* empty x = None = Some T' *) discriminate. }
  (* CR -> SN_f *)
  pose proof (CR_ok_sn T t HCR) as HSNf.
  (* SN_f -> SN *)
  ...
Qed.
```

```
Lemma CR_app_abs : forall u, SN_f u ->
  forall T1 T2 body,
    (forall w, CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open body w)) ->
    CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).

Proof: intros u HSN; induction HSN as [s Hs]; intros T1 T2 body Hbody HCRu. apply CR_ok_cand T2.

Let's write:

```coq
Inductive fstep : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  | F_AppAbs : forall T t u,
      fstep (tm_app (tm_abs T t) u) (open t u)
  | F_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2,
      fstep t1 t1' ->
      fstep (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2)
  | F_App2 : forall t1 t2 t2',
      fstep t2 t2' ->
      fstep (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1 t2')
  | F_IfTrue : forall t1 t2,
      fstep (tm_if tm_true t1 t2) t1
  | F_IfFalse : forall t1 t2,
      fstep (tm_if tm_false t1 t2) t2
  | F_If : forall t1 t1' t2 t3,
      fstep t1 t1' ->
      fstep (tm_if t1 t2 t3) (tm_if t1' t2 t3).

Inductive sn_f : tm -> Prop :=
  | sn_f_intro : forall t,
      (forall t', fstep t t' -> sn_f t') ->
      sn_f t.

Lemma step_fstep : forall t t', step t t' -> fstep t t'.
Proof. intros t t' H; induction H; econstructor; eauto. Qed.
```

Wait for ST_AppAbs, conclusion step (app (abs T t) v) (open t v); F_AppAbs conclusion fstep (app (abs T t) u) (open t u) — exact. `econstructor; eauto` might work but constructors have different names; `econstructor` picks the matching one? For each case, `econstructor` tries constructors; for ST_AppAbs, F_AppAbs matches with no premises. But econstructor with eauto should work. Let me list cases:
- ST_AppAbs: econstructor.
- ST_App1: econstructor; eauto (premise IH).
- ST_App2: econstructor; eauto.
- ST_IfTrue: econstructor.
- ST_IfFalse: econstructor.
- ST_If: econstructor; eauto.
`induction H; econstructor; eauto` might over-apply in some cases. Safer to write explicit:

```
Proof.
  intros t t' H. induction H.
  - apply F_AppAbs.
  - apply F_App1; assumption.
  - apply F_App2; assumption.
  - apply F_IfTrue.
  - apply F_IfFalse.
  - apply F_If; assumption.
Qed.
```

```
Lemma sn_f_sn : forall t, sn_f t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t H. induction H as [s Hs].
  apply SN_intro. intros s' Hstep.
  apply Hs. apply step_fstep. exact Hstep.
Qed.

Definition neutral (t : tm) : Prop :=

fv:

```
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

subst and extend:

```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 =>
      tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition extend (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
```

Lemma subst_open_rec : forall t k u sigma,
  subst sigma (open_rec k u t) =
  open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  induction t; intros k u sigma; simpl; auto.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n); auto.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3. reflexivity.
Qed.
```

In tm_bvar case: open_rec k u (tm_bvar n) = if eqb k n then u else bvar n; subst of that = if eqb k n then subst sigma u else bvar n; RHS open_rec k (subst sigma u) (bvar n) = same. destruct eqb; auto. ✓. But careful: after `induction t`, the names of variables; use `destruct (Nat.eqb k n)`. The induction's k is intros'd. Let me set the statement order to `forall t k u sigma` and proof `induction t; intros k u sigma; simpl; auto.` In bvar case, goal: subst sigma (if Nat.eqb k n then u else tm_bvar n) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (tm_bvar n). simpl might reduce both to if...; destruct. For abs: goal involves open_rec (S k) in IH; IHt : forall k u sigma, ...; apply IHt with (k := S k). `simpl; rewrite IHt; reflexivity` should work since the goal after simpl is tm_abs T (subst sigma (open_rec (S k) u t)) = tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t))? Fixpoint sum_list (L : list atom) : nat :=

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (sum_list L).

Lemma fresh_gt : forall L x, In x L -> x < fresh L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl in Hin. simpl. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].

Chunk 1:

```coq
(** * Auxiliary development for the strong normalization proof. *)

(** Full-beta reduction, a superset of the call-by-value step relation. *)

Inductive fstep : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  | F_AppAbs : forall T t u,
      fstep (tm_app (tm_abs T t) u) (open t u)
  | F_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2,
      fstep t1 t1' ->
      fstep (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2)
  | F_App2 : forall t1 t2 t2',
      fstep t2 t2' ->
      fstep (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1 t2')
  | F_IfTrue : forall t1 t2,
      fstep (tm_if tm_true t1 t2) t1
  | F_IfFalse : forall t1 t2,
      fstep (tm_if tm_false t1 t2) t2
  | F_If : forall t1 t1' t2 t3,
      fstep t1 t1' ->
      fstep (tm_if t1 t2 t3) (tm_if t1' t2 t3).

Inductive sn_f : tm -> Prop :=
  | sn_f_intro : forall t,
      (forall t', fstep t t' -> sn_f t') ->
      sn_f t.

Lemma step_fstep : forall t t', step t t' -> fstep t t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' H. induction H.
  - apply F_AppAbs.
  - apply F_App1; assumption.
  - apply F_App2; assumption.
  - apply F_IfTrue.
  - apply F_IfFalse.
  - apply F_If; assumption.
Qed.

Lemma sn_f_sn : forall t, sn_f t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t H. induction H as [s Hs].
  apply SN_intro. intros s' Hstep.
  apply Hs. apply step_fstep. exact Hstep.
Qed.
```

Now add the CR part. Let me write the definitions and CR_ok_all. I'll write it step by step.

First the neutral definition and CR.

```
Definition neutral (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_abs _ _ => False
  | _ => True
  end.

Fixpoint CR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => sn_f t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => forall u, CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.

Definition CR_ok (T : ty) : Prop :=
  (forall t, CR T t -> sn_f t) /\
  (forall t t', CR T t -> fstep t t' -> CR T t') /\
  (forall t, neutral t -> (forall t', fstep t t' -> CR T t') -> CR T t) /\
  (forall k, CR T (tm_bvar k)).
```

Then sn_f_app_l (as derived).

For P3's inversion, let me first write a dedicated inversion lemma in a simple, robust style:

```
Lemma fstep_app_inv : forall t1 t2 s',
  fstep (tm_app t1 t2) s' ->
  (exists T body, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ s' = open body t2) \/
  (exists t1', fstep t1 t1' /\ s' = tm_app t1' t2) \/
  (exists t2', fstep t2 t2' /\ s' = tm_app t1 t2').

  - (* F_AppAbs *) left. eexists. eexists. split; reflexivity... 
```
Hmm, in the F_AppAbs case, inversion may have substituted t1 and t2. Need to see.

Alternatively, avoid inversion lemmas entirely: use `generalize dependent` + `induction Hstep` and case-analysis on the LHS? No.

Another idea: Since `fstep` is a relation defined by constructors, I can prove the inversion lemma by `remember` of LHS and `induction`:

```
Lemma fstep_app_inv : forall t1 t2 s', fstep (tm_app t1 t2) s' -> ...
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 s' H.
  remember (tm_app t1 t2) as a eqn:E.
  revert t1 t2 E.
  induction H; intros t1 t2 E; injection E as E1 E2; subst; ...
```
This is robust: induction on H (all 6 cases), then use injectivity of tm_app to decompose E. For F_AppAbs case: H : fstep (tm_app (tm_abs T t) u) (open t u), E : tm_app (tm_abs T t) u = tm_app t1 t2. injection E as E1 E2 gives tm_abs T t = t1 and u = t2; subst t1 t2. Goal: left; exists T, t; split; reflexivity. ✓.
F_App1: H : fstep (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2) with premise fstep t1 t1'. E : tm_app t1 t2 = tm_app t1_0 t2_0 (the outer variables); injection gives t1 = t1_0, t2 = t2_0; subst. Goal: right; left; exists t1'; split; [assumption | reflexivity]. ✓.
F_App2: similar.
F_IfTrue: E : tm_if ... = tm_app t1 t2, `injection E` or `discriminate E` closes. Since tm_if vs tm_app are different constructors, injection E gives "false" or discriminates. `injection E` might fail because constructors differ; `discriminate E` solves. Similarly F_IfFalse, F_If.

Wait, but induction on H where H : fstep (tm_app t1 t2) s' — after remember of the LHS as a, induction on H gives cases where the LHS is the constructor's LHS: e.g., F_AppAbs case has LHS tm_app (tm_abs T t) u; E : tm_app (tm_abs T t) u = a? Let's set up: `remember (tm_app t1 t2) as a eqn:E` gives E : tm_app t1 t2 = a. Then `revert t1 t2 E; induction H` — the motive: fun a s' => forall t1 t2, tm_app t1 t2 = a -> ... . In the F_AppAbs case: a = tm_app (tm_abs T t) u, s' = open t u; E : tm_app t1 t2 = tm_app (tm_abs T t) u; injection E as ...; subst; left; exists. ✓.

Note: the F_IfTrue case: a = tm_if true t1 t2; E : tm_app t1_0 t2_0 = tm_if true t1 t2; discriminate E. ✓. But naming conflicts.

This approach is quite robust. Let me use it for fstep_app_inv and fstep_if_inv.

Actually, do we need fstep_if_inv? Yes, in CR_if. Let me define:

```
Lemma fstep_if_inv : forall t1 t2 t3 s',
  fstep (tm_if t1 t2 t3) s' ->
  (t1 = tm_true /\ s' = t2) \/ (t1 = tm_false /\ s' = t3) \/
  (exists t1', fstep t1 t1' /\ s' = tm_if t1' t2 t3).
```

Proof with remember/induction:
- F_AppAbs: a = tm_app ...; E : tm_if t1 t2 t3 = tm_app ...; discriminate.
- F_App1/2: discriminate.
- F_IfTrue: a = tm_if tm_true t1 t2, s' = t1 (careful naming); E : tm_if t1_0 t2_0 t3_0 = tm_if tm_true t1 t2; injection gives t1_0 = tm_true, t2_0 = t1, t3_0 = t2. Goal: (tm_true = tm_true /\ t1 = t1) \/ ...; left; auto.
- F_IfFalse: similar.
- F_If: a = tm_if t1 t2 t3 with premise fstep t1 t1'; E gives t1_0 = t1, t2_0 = t2, t3_0 = t3; goal: right; right; exists t1'; auto.

And fstep_abs_inv:
```
Lemma fstep_abs_inv : forall T t s', ~ fstep (tm_abs T t) s'.
Proof.
  intros T t s' H. remember (tm_abs T t) as a eqn:E. revert T t E.
  induction H; intros T0 t0 E; try discriminate E.
Qed.
```
Hmm, in each case E is an equation between tm_abs ... and the constructor LHS (app/if), so discriminate. But `induction H` with remember: the motive must include T,t. Let's write: `intros T t s' H. remember (tm_abs T t) as a eqn:E. revert T t E. induction H; intros T0 t0 E; discriminate E.` Each case: F_AppAbs has E : tm_abs T0 t0 = tm_app (tm_abs T t) u? ```
intros k u HCRu.
assert (Haux : forall u, sn_f u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app (tm_bvar k) u)).

For the `remember ... induction` proof style, let me write fstep_app_inv:

```
Lemma fstep_app_inv : forall t1 t2 s',
  fstep (tm_app t1 t2) s' ->
  (exists T body, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ s' = open body t2) \/
  (exists t1', fstep t1 t1' /\ s' = tm_app t1' t2) \/
  (exists t2', fstep t2 t2' /\ s' = tm_app t1 t2').
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 s' H.
  remember (tm_app t1 t2) as a eqn:E.
  revert t1 t2 E.
  induction H as [T t u | a1 a1' a2 Hst | a1 a2 a2' Hst | b1 b2 | b1 b2 | a1 a1' a2 a3 Hst];
    intros t1 t2 E; try discriminate E.
  - injection E as E1 E2. subst t1. subst t2.
    left. exists T, t. split; reflexivity.
  - injection E as E1 E2. subst t1. subst t2.
    right. left. exists a1'. split; assumption.
  - injection E as E1 E2. subst t1. subst t2.
    right. right. exists a2'. split; assumption.
Qed.
```

F_App2 t1 t2 t2' Hst

F_IfFalse t1 t2
F_If t1 t1' t2 t3 Hst

```
Lemma fstep_if_inv : forall t1 t2 t3 s',
  fstep (tm_if t1 t2 t3) s' ->
  (t1 = tm_true /\ s' = t2) \/ (t1 = tm_false /\ s' = t3) \/

  revert t1 t2 t3 E.

    left. split; reflexivity.

    right. left. split; reflexivity.
  - injection E as E1 E2 E3. subst t1. subst t2. subst t3.
    right. right. exists a1'. split; [assumption | reflexivity].
Qed.
```

Define:
```
Fixpoint CR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ sn_f t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => lc_at 0 t /\ (forall u, CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app t u))

```

This adds substantial work: typing_lc lemma and lc substitution lemmas. Let me consider whether there's an alternative that avoids the lc requirement.

Alternative Option B: Change the definition of `subst`/opening or the logical relation to avoid the commutation issue.

The problematic identity: subst σ (open t u) = open (subst σ t) (subst σ u). It fails when subst σ u has free bvars that get captured under the opening. This is a fundamental locally nameless issue: open_rec is only safe for lc arguments. In the T_Abs case, the argument u is arbitrary CR T1.

Could we instead define the logical relation on *pre-closed* terms with respect to bvars? I.e., define a variant CR' where t is lc_at 0. That's Option A.

Alternative Option C: Use a substitution that maps atoms to terms and *reindex* bvars? The issue is that open_rec doesn't shift inserted terms. If we used a capture-avoiding opening (with shifting), things would be different. But `open` is given as open_rec 0 with no shifting. However, note: our fstep relation uses `open t u`, and the typing rule uses `open t1 (fvar x)`. The language definitions are fixed.

Alternative Option D: Strengthen the fundamental theorem's σ range to lc terms, and additionally define the logical relation arrow clause only for *lc* arguments? I.e., CR arrow t := lc t ∧ forall u, lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (app t u)? Then u is lc by hypothesis, solving the substitution issue. But then CR_sn at arrow needs an lc argument in CR T1; use fvar x (need CR_fvar with the lc-condition variant). The app condition in P1 etc. would quantify over lc u only, but we provide lc u. Let me explore:

Define CR T t := lc_at 0 t /\ [core T t], with
core Bool t := sn_f t
core (T1→T2) t := forall u, lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (app t u).

```
Fixpoint CR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=

Now the fundamental theorem:
- σ must map atoms to lc terms (for the subst_open commutation). Context realization includes this.
- T_Abs case: body = subst σ t1. Need CR (T1→T2) (abs T1 body): for lc u, CR T1 u, need CR T2 (app (abs T1 body) u). Use CR_app_abs lemma: 
  CR_app_abs : forall u, sn_f u -> forall T1 T2 body,
    (forall w, lc_at 0 w -> CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open body w)) ->

  Proof by SN induction on u, using CR_cand at T2 on app (abs T1 body) u: neutral ✓; reducts:

    F_App2 → u --> u'; SN-IH with lc u' (fstep_lc0), CR T1 u' (P2). ✓.
  Great: F_AppAbs in fstep has no lc premise, so we don't need lc of the abstraction! 

So we don't need lc_at 0 (abs T1 body) at all! Excellent. The lc requirement is only on the *argument* terms involved in substitution/opening. And where do we need lc arguments?
  - In CR arrow clause: arguments u must be lc.

```
Lemma CR_app_abs : forall u, sn_f u -> forall T1 T2 body,
  (forall w, lc_at 0 w -> CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open body w)) ->
  lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).

  intros T1 T2 body Hbody Hlc HCRu.
  apply CR_cand.
  - simpl. exact I.
  - intros s' Hstep.
    destruct (fstep_app_inv (tm_abs T1 body) s s' Hstep) as
      [[T0 [body0 [Heq1 Heq2]]] | [[t1' [Hst Heq]] | [s2 [Hst Heq]]]].

    + subst s'. apply IH; [exact Hst | exact Hbody | apply fstep_lc0 with (t := s); assumption | apply CR_step with (t := s); assumption].

Qed.
```

The induction on H : lc_at 0 (abs T t1) is on a derivation of lc_at 0 ..., whose subderivations are lc_at 1, lc_at 2, etc. The property "open_rec k y t = t for all k y" is not the same at level 1 as at level 0 (a term lc_at 1 may have bvar 0 free; open_rec 0 y would replace it!). So induction on the derivation doesn't directly work with a fixed level-0 statement.

Better statement: forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall j y, open_rec j y t = t when j... hmm. For a term lc_at k with free bvars < k, open_rec j y t = t if j does not coincide with any free bvar index... Actually open_rec j replaces bvar j occurrences. If t is lc_at k, bvars i with i < k occur; under binders, the level increases. The net condition for open_rec j to do nothing is: for every bvar i at depth d (i < k + d), j ≠ i. Hmm.

We need open_rec k y x = x for x lc_at 0. A cleaner general lemma: open_rec_rec with level invariance: if lc_at k t and j < k, hmm no: the depth increases with binders, so free bvars at depth d are < k+d; open_rec j at depth d replaces index j, but the relevant comparison is j+d? Let's look at the definition: open_rec k u t replaces bvar i when Nat.eqb k i, and under binders uses S k. So at depth d, the target index is k+d. A bvar occurrence i at depth d in t is bound iff i < d (roughly); the free ones are ≥ d. For lc_at 0 t, free bvars don't exist, so i < d at every occurrence; target k+d ≥ d > i, so never replaced. So the criterion for open_rec k u t = t is: every bvar i at depth d satisfies i ≠ k+d. For lc_at 0 t, i < d ≤ k+d, so ✓.

That works: induction on the term t, with the lc_at 0 hypothesis; then in the abs case, lc_at 0 (abs T t1) gives lc_at 1 t1, and we need open_rec (S j) y t1 = t1, which is a statement about lc_at 1, not 0. Hmm, let's think: t lc_at k means bvars at depth d are < k + d. open_rec j at depth d targets index j + d. If j ≥ k, then j + d ≥ k + d > i, so no replacement. So the condition is j ≥ k. Proof by induction on t:
- bvar i: H : lc_at k (bvar i) gives i < k. open_rec j y (bvar i) = if eqb j i then y else bvar i. Since i < k ≤ j, i ≠ j; eqb false. ✓.

- app/if/fvar/true/false ✓.

The issue is capture: we need the substituted terms to have no free bvars at the relevant index. What if we require "no free bvar 0"? Under opening at level 0, only bvar 0 causes capture. But under nested opens (inside a body), the level differs. Hmm, our substitution σ maps atoms to terms inserted at whatever binder depth; the failure arises when the inserted term has a free bvar equal to the opening level... Actually with the subst_open_rec lemma, the problematic issue is that open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ t) can capture free bvars of subst σ u (at index k) inside σ a. Could we instead define the environment to map to terms with no free bvars, i.e., lc_at 0, but not require CR arguments in the arrow clause to be lc? ```
fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T ->

where env_ok Gamma sigma := (forall a, lc_at 0 (sigma a)) /\ (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> CR T (sigma x)).

T_Abs case: Gamma, T1, T2, t1, L, Hprem. Goal: CR (T1→T2) (subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)). Simplifies to forall u, lc u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (app (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) u).
intros u Hlcu HCRu.
Choose x := fresh (L ++ fv t1). HxL : ~ In x L, Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1).
Define sigma_w for arbitrary w: extend sigma x w.
Prove Hbody : forall w, lc_at 0 w -> CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open (subst sigma t1) w).
  intros w Hlcw HCRw.
  assert (Henv : env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (extend sigma x w)).
  { split.
    - intros a. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x a) eqn:E.
      + exact Hlcw.
      + apply Hsigma_lc.  (* from env_ok sigma *)
    - intros y T' Hy. simpl in Hy. unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
      + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. injection Hy as Hy'. subst T'. 
        unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact HCRw.
      + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. unfold extend. rewrite E. apply Hsigma_CR. exact Hy. }
  specialize (Hprem_IH x HxL (extend sigma x w) Henv). 
  (* Hprem_IH : forall sigma, env_ok ... -> CR T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))) *)
  rewrite subst_open_rec_lc in Hprem_IH.
    (* subst (extend sigma x w) (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1)
       = open_rec 0 (subst (extend ...) (fvar x)) (subst (extend ...) t1)
       = open_rec 0 w (subst (extend ...) t1) *)

  rewrite subst_extend_neq in Hprem_IH.  (* x ∉ fv t1 *)
  exact Hprem_IH.
Then apply CR_app_abs with u HSNu ... where HSNu := P1 T1 u HCRu.
  apply (CR_app_abs u (CR_sn T1 u HCRu) T1 T2 (subst sigma t1) Hbody Hlcu HCRu).

Lemma subst_open_rec_lc : forall t k u sigma,
  (forall a, lc_at 0 (sigma a)) ->
  subst sigma (open_rec k u t) =

```
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k1 t, lc_at k1 t -> forall k2, k1 <= k2 -> lc_at k2 t.
Proof.
  intros k1 t H. induction H; intros k2 Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IHlc_at1. exact Hle.
    + apply IHlc_at2. exact Hle.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IHlc_at. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if.
    + apply IHlc_at1. exact Hle.
    + apply IHlc_at2. exact Hle.
    + apply IHlc_at3. exact Hle.

```

The premises are named lc_at1? Coq auto-names hypotheses from the constructor's argument names, which in the inductive definition are `lc_at k t1` and `lc_at k t2` with no explicit names... The constructor is:
```
| lc_app : forall k t1 t2, lc_at k t1 -> lc_at k t2 -> lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2)
```
No names for the premises, so Coq auto-names them lc_at0? or H? Then the IH names might be IHlc_at0 etc. To be safe, use explicit intro patterns:

```
  intros k1 t H. induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 H2 IH1 IH2 | k T1 t1 H1 IH1 | k | k | k t1 t2 t3 H1 H2 H3 IH1 IH2 IH3]; intros k2 Hle.
```
With induction on an inductive with index k in the goal, the intro pattern names: first the constructor's arguments, and for each recursive premise, the IH is generated as an extra name after? In the sn_f test, the pattern [s Hs IH] had 3 names for 2 arguments (the recursive arg produced both the original Hs and the IH). For lc_at, the constructor lc_app has args k t1 t2, premises H1 : lc_at k t1 and H2 : lc_at k t2. The induction principle will give (k t1 t2 H1 H2 IH1 IH2)? Let me test in /tmp.

(** ** Closure properties of the locally-nameless level predicate. *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k1 t, lc_at k1 t -> forall k2, k1 <= k2 -> lc_at k2 t.
Proof.
  intros k1 t H. induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k a b H1 IH1 H2 IH2 |
                                  k T b H1 IH1 | k | k | k a b c H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3];
    intros k2 Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; [apply IH1 | apply IH2]; exact Hle.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH1. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; [apply IH1 | apply IH2 | apply IH3]; exact Hle.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_weaken : forall k t, lc_at k t -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof. intros. apply lc_at_mono with (k1 := k); [exact H | lia]. Qed.
```

```
Lemma open_rec_lc_gen : forall t k, lc_at k t ->
  forall j y, k <= j -> open_rec j y t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros k Hlc j y Hkj; simpl; auto.
  - (* bvar n *) pose proof (lc_at_bvar_inv k n Hlc). 
    destruct (Nat.eqb j n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. lia.
    + reflexivity.
  - (* app *) apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc. destruct Hlc as [H1 H2].
    rewrite IHt1; [rewrite IHt2; reflexivity | exact H1 | exact Hkj | exact H2 | exact Hkj].
  ...
```
Wait, the goal after simpl for app: tm_app (open_rec j y t1) (open_rec j y t2) = tm_app t1 t2. rewrite IHt1 with k H1 j y Hkj; rewrite IHt2. The order of arguments for IHt1: IHt1 : forall k, lc_at k t1 -> forall j y, k <= j -> open_rec j y t1 = t1. So `rewrite (IHt1 k H1 j y Hkj)` then `rewrite (IHt2 k H2 j y Hkj)`; reflexivity.

- abs: Hlc : lc_at k (abs T t1) → lc_at (S k) t1. Goal: tm_abs T (open_rec (S j) y t1) = tm_abs T t1; rewrite (IHt1 (S k) H1 (S j) y); need S k <= S j ↔ k <= j (lia). 
- true/false: auto.
- fvar: auto (simpl gives tm_fvar a = tm_fvar a).
- if: similar to app.

Careful with the auto at the beginning: after `induction t; intros k Hlc j y Hkj; simpl; auto.` — auto might solve fvar/true/false cases. Good.

So:
```
Lemma open_rec_lc_gen : forall t k, lc_at k t ->
  forall j y, k <= j -> open_rec j y t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros k Hlc j y Hkj; simpl; auto.
  - pose proof (lc_at_bvar_inv k n Hlc). 
    destruct (Nat.eqb j n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. lia.
    + reflexivity.
  - apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc. destruct Hlc as [H1 H2].
    rewrite (IHt1 k H1 j y Hkj). rewrite (IHt2 k H2 j y Hkj). reflexivity.
  - apply lc_at_abs_inv in Hlc.
    rewrite (IHt1 (S k) Hlc (S j) y). reflexivity. lia.
  - apply lc_at_if_inv in Hlc. destruct Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]].
    rewrite (IHt1 k H1 j y Hkj). rewrite (IHt2 k H2 j y Hkj).
    rewrite (IHt3 k H3 j y Hkj). reflexivity.
Qed.
```
In the abs case, rewrite (IHt1 (S k) Hlc (S j) y) leaves the side condition S k <= S j, which we prove with lia. But the rewrite might not know to generate the side goal in that position; `rewrite (...)` with a partially applied lemma: `rewrite (IHt1 (S k) Hlc (S j) y)` has type `S k <= S j -> open_rec (S j) y t1 = t1`; rewrite will use it and create the side condition. Then `reflexivity` and `lia` ordering: after rewrite, goal is tm_abs T t1 = tm_abs T t1 (side condition solved by rewriting? Actually rewrite with a conditional lemma produces the side condition as a new goal, and the main goal becomes t1 = t1). So `rewrite (...); [reflexivity | lia]`. Let me write it as:
```
    rewrite (IHt1 (S k) Hlc (S j) y); [reflexivity | lia].
```
Similarly others.

Hmm, for `rewrite (IHt1 k H1 j y Hkj)`: fully applied, no side condition. ✓.

Lemma lc_open_inv : forall t k u, lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t.

Careful: `simpl in H` for the fvar case: H : lc_at k (tm_fvar a) → goal lc_at (S k) (tm_fvar a) = lc_fvar ✓ (auto using lc_fvar).
- bvar: destruct eqb.
- app: apply lc_at_app_inv in H; destruct; apply lc_app; [apply IHt1 with u | apply IHt2 with u]; assumption.
- abs: apply lc_at_abs_inv in H. Then H : lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1). Apply IHt1 with (k := S k) (u := u) in H → lc_at (S (S k)) t1. Apply lc_abs. exact H.

lc_open_rec:
```
Lemma lc_open_rec : forall t k u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  induction t; intros k u Ht Hu; simpl; auto using lc_fvar, lc_true, lc_false.
  - (* bvar *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. exact Hu.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply lc_at_bvar_inv in Ht. lia.
  - (* app *) apply lc_at_app_inv in Ht. destruct Ht as [H1 H2]. apply lc_app.
    + apply IHt1 with u; assumption.
    + apply IHt2 with u; assumption.
  - (* abs *) apply lc_at_abs_inv in Ht.
    apply lc_abs. apply IHt1 with (k := S k) (u := u).
    + exact Ht.
    + apply lc_at_weaken. exact Hu.
  - (* if *) apply lc_at_if_inv in Ht. destruct Ht as [H1 [H2 H3]]. apply lc_if.
    + apply IHt1 with u; assumption.
    + apply IHt2 with u; assumption.
    + apply IHt3 with u; assumption.
Qed.
```
For the fvar case, `auto using lc_fvar` should solve lc_at k (tm_fvar a) — note simpl: open_rec k u (tm_fvar a) = tm_fvar a. ✓. For true/false similar.

Wait, the bvar case when eqb is false: goal lc_at k (tm_bvar n); need n < k. Ht : lc_at (S k) (tm_bvar n) → n < S k. With E : eqb k n = false → k ≠ n. From n < S k and n ≠ k, n < k. lia. ✓. Need to get E to lia: Nat.eqb_neq. Let me add `apply Nat.eqb_neq in E` then lia.

fstep_lc:

Lemma fstep_lc : forall t t', lc_at 0 t -> fstep t t' -> lc_at 0 t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hlc Hstep. revert Hlc. induction Hstep; intros Hlc.
  - (* F_AppAbs *) apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc. destruct Hlc as [Habs Hu].
    apply lc_at_abs_inv in Habs.
    unfold open. apply lc_open_rec with (u := u)... 
```

    exact (lc_open_rec t 0 u Habs Hu). But the goal is lc_at 0 (open t u) = lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 u t). unfold open; exact.
  - F_App1: Hlc : lc_at 0 (app t1 t2); lc app inv → H1, H2. IH : lc_at 0 t1 -> lc_at 0 t1' (since premise fstep t1 t1'). apply IH, H1. Then lc_app with result. 
    `apply lc_app. + apply IHHstep. exact H1. + exact H2.` But IHHstep's name depends on the premise name; with `induction Hstep` and explicit intro patterns, I can name things. Let me use:
    `induction Hstep as [T body u | a1 a1' a2 Hst1 IH1 | a1 a2 a2' Hst2 IH2 | b1 b2 | b1 b2 | a1 a1' a2 a3 Hst1 IH1]; intros Hlc.`

    F_IfTrue b1 b2.
    F_IfFalse b1 b2.
    F_If a1 a1' a2 a3 Hst1; IH1.

  - F_IfFalse ✓.
  - F_If: lc_at_if_inv gives lc a1, a2, a3; IH1 H_lc_a1 : lc_at 0 a1'; goal lc_at 0 (if a1' a2 a3); lc_if ✓.

Now, the fundamental theorem with env_ok. Let me define:

```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall a, lc_at 0 (sigma a)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> CR T (sigma x)).
```

Now the final theorem:
```
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (HCR : CR T t).
  { apply (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
    split.
    - intros a. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T' Hx. simpl in Hx. discriminate Hx. }
  apply sn_f_sn. apply CR_sn with (T := T). exact HCR.
Qed.
```
Wait, `subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t` should equal t, not syntactically t. The fundamental theorem gives CR T (subst sigma t) with sigma = identity; we need CR T t. So we need `subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t`. Then rewrite. Let me add that lemma:
```

New section:

```coq
Fixpoint CR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => sn_f t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => forall u, lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.

Definition CR_ok (T : ty) : Prop :=
  (forall t, CR T t -> sn_f t) /\
  (forall t t', CR T t -> fstep t t' -> CR T t') /\
  (forall t, neutral t -> (forall t', fstep t t' -> CR T t') -> CR T t) /\
  (forall x, CR T (tm_fvar x)).

Lemma sn_f_app_l : forall t u, sn_f (tm_app t u) -> sn_f t.
Proof.
  intros t u H. remember (tm_app t u) as s eqn:E. revert t u E.
  induction H as [s Hs IH]; intros t u E; subst s.
  apply sn_f_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  exact (IH (tm_app t' u) (F_App1 Hstep) t' u eq_refl).
Qed.

Lemma CR_ok_all : forall T, CR_ok T.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2].
  - unfold CR_ok. simpl. split.
    + intros t H; exact H.
    + split.
      * intros t t' H Hstep. destruct H as [s Hs]. apply Hs. exact Hstep.
      * split.
        -- intros t Hn Hred. apply sn_f_intro. exact Hred.
        -- intros x. apply sn_f_intro. intros t' Hstep.
           exact (fstep_fvar_inv x t' Hstep).
  - destruct IH1 as [IH1_sn [IH1_step [IH1_cand IH1_fvar]]].
    destruct IH2 as [IH2_sn [IH2_step [IH2_cand IH2_fvar]]].
    unfold CR_ok. simpl. split.
    + intros t HCR. apply sn_f_app_l with (u := tm_fvar 0).
      apply IH2_sn. apply HCR. apply lc_fvar. apply IH1_fvar.
    + split.
      * intros t t' HCR Hstep u Hlcu HCRu.
        apply IH2_step with (t := tm_app t u).
        -- apply HCR. exact Hlcu. exact HCRu.
        -- apply F_App1. exact Hstep.
      * split.
        -- intros t Hn Hred u Hlcu HCRu.
           assert (Haux : forall u, sn_f u -> lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app t u)).
           { intros u0 HSN. induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
             intros Hlc HCRs.
             apply IH2_cand.
             ++ simpl. exact I.
             ++ intros s' Hstep.
                destruct (fstep_app_inv t s s' Hstep) as
                  [[T0 [body [Heq1 Heq2]]] | [[t1' [Hst Heq]] | [s2 [Hst Heq]]]].
                ** subst t. unfold neutral in Hn. simpl in Hn. destruct Hn.
                ** subst s'. apply (Hred t1' Hst u Hlc HCRs).
                ** subst s'. apply IH.
                   --- exact Hst.
                   --- apply fstep_lc with (t := s); assumption.
                   --- apply IH1_step with (t := s); assumption.
           }
           apply Haux.
           ++ apply IH1_sn. exact HCRu.
           ++ exact Hlcu.
           ++ exact HCRu.
        -- intros x u Hlcu HCRu.
           assert (Haux : forall u, sn_f u -> lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app (tm_fvar x) u)).
           { intros u0 HSN. induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
             intros Hlc HCRs.
             apply IH2_cand.
             ++ simpl. exact I.
             ++ intros s' Hstep.
                destruct (fstep_app_inv (tm_fvar x) s s' Hstep) as
                  [[T0 [body [Heq1 Heq2]]] | [[t1' [Hst Heq]] | [s2 [Hst Heq]]]].
                ** discriminate Heq1.
                ** subst s'. exfalso. exact (fstep_fvar_inv x t1' Hst).
                ** subst s'. apply IH.
                   --- exact Hst.
                   --- apply fstep_lc with (t := s); assumption.
                   --- apply IH1_step with (t := s); assumption.
           }
           apply Haux.
           ++ apply IH1_sn. exact HCRu.
           ++ exact Hlcu.
           ++ exact HCRu.
Qed.

Lemma CR_sn : forall T t, CR T t -> sn_f t.
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [H _]. exact H. Qed.

Lemma CR_step : forall T t t', CR T t -> fstep t t' -> CR T t'.
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [_ [H _]]. exact H. Qed.

Lemma CR_cand : forall T t, neutral t ->
  (forall t', fstep t t' -> CR T t') -> CR T t.
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [_ [_ [H _]]]. exact H. Qed.

Lemma CR_fvar : forall T x, CR T (tm_fvar x).
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [_ [_ [_ H]]]. exact H. Qed.

(** If [body] is reducible at [T2] after opening with any locally closed
    reducible argument, then [tm_abs T1 body] applied to such an argument
    is reducible at [T2]. *)

Lemma CR_app_abs : forall u, sn_f u -> forall T1 T2 body,
  (forall w, lc_at 0 w -> CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open body w)) ->
  lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros u HSN. induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
  intros T1 T2 body Hbody Hlc HCRu.
  apply CR_cand.
  - simpl. exact I.
  - intros s' Hstep.
    destruct (fstep_app_inv (tm_abs T1 body) s s' Hstep) as
      [[T0 [body0 [Heq1 Heq2]]] | [[t1' [Hst Heq]] | [s2 [Hst Heq]]]].
    + injection Heq1 as E1 E2. subst T0. subst body0. subst s'.
      apply Hbody; assumption.
    + subst s'. exfalso. exact (fstep_abs_inv T1 body t1' Hst).
    + subst s'. apply IH.
      * exact Hst.
      * exact Hbody.
      * apply fstep_lc with (t := s); assumption.
      * apply CR_step with (t := s); assumption.
Qed.

(** Reducibility of conditionals. *)

Lemma CR_if : forall T t1 t2 t3,
  sn_f t1 -> CR T t2 -> CR T t3 -> CR T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros T t1 t2 t3 HSN. revert T t2 t3.
  induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
  intros T t2 t3 H2 H3.
  apply CR_cand.
  - simpl. exact I.
  - intros s' Hstep.
    destruct (fstep_if_inv s t2 t3 s' Hstep) as
      [[E1 E2] | [[E1 E2] | [s0 [Hst Heq]]]].
    + subst s. subst s'. exact H2.
    + subst s. subst s'. exact H3.
    + subst s'. apply IH; assumption.
Qed.

(** ** The fundamental theorem. *)

Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall a, lc_at 0 (sigma a)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> CR T (sigma x)).

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  <{ Gamma |-- t \in T }> ->
  forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> CR T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty. induction Hty as
    [Gamma x T0 Hx
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IHprem
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma
    | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T0 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply Hcr... 
```
Wait, the intros pattern: after `intros Gamma t T Hty`, the goal is forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> CR T (subst sigma t). The induction motive P Gamma t T := forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> CR T (subst sigma t). So in each case, after the constructor args, the goal is P ..., and I should `intros sigma Henv` in each case. Since I did `induction Hty as [...]`, each goal is P ...; I can `intros sigma Henv` per case, or `simpl` first. Let me write each case with intros.

- T_Var: `intros sigma [Hlc Hcr]. simpl. apply Hcr. exact Hx.`
- T_Abs: `intros sigma [Hlc Hcr]. simpl. intros u Hlcu HCRu. ...`
- T_App: `intros sigma [Hlc Hcr]. simpl. ...`
- T_True/T_False/T_If similarly.

Note: `intros sigma [Hlc Hcr]` destructs env_ok (a conjunction) directly in intros; works if env_ok is a Definition (unfolded by intros? `intros [Hlc Hcr]` requires the goal's premise to be a conjunction; env_ok is defined as a conjunction, and Coq's intro pattern `[..]` does delta-reduction to expose the conjunction? I think it works (it unfolds definitions for the pattern). To be safe, `intros sigma Henv; destruct Henv as [Hlc Hcr]`.

Now, T_Abs proof details:

```
  - (* T_Abs *)
    intros sigma Henv. destruct Henv as [Hlc Hcr].
    simpl. intros u Hlcu HCRu.
    pose (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
    { intro Hin. pose proof (fresh_gt (L ++ fv t1) x (in_or_app L (fv t1) x (or_introl Hin))). lia. }

```

Simpler:
```
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
    { intro Hin. assert (Hin' : In x (L ++ fv t1)) by (apply in_or_app; left; exact Hin).
      pose proof (fresh_gt (L ++ fv t1) x Hin'). lia. }
    assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1)).
    { intro Hin. assert (Hin' : In x (L ++ fv t1)) by (apply in_or_app; right; exact Hin).
      pose proof (fresh_gt (L ++ fv t1) x Hin'). lia. }
    assert (Hbody : forall w, lc_at 0 w -> CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open (subst sigma t1) w)).
    { intros w Hlcw HCRw.
      assert (Henv : env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (extend sigma x w)).
      { split.
        - intros a. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x a) eqn:E.
          + exact Hlcw.
          + exact Hlc.
        - intros y T' Hy. unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
          + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y.
            injection Hy as Hy'. subst T'.
            unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact HCRw.
          + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. unfold extend. rewrite E.
            apply Hcr. exact Hy. }
      destruct Henv as [Henv_lc Henv_cr].
      specialize (IHprem x HxL (extend sigma x w) (conj Henv_lc Henv_cr)).
      rewrite (subst_open_rec_lc t1 0 (tm_fvar x) (extend sigma x w) Henv_lc) in IHprem.
      rewrite subst_extend_same in IHprem.
      rewrite (subst_extend_neq t1 sigma x w Hxfv) in IHprem.
      unfold open in IHprem. exact IHprem. }
    apply (CR_app_abs u (CR_sn T1 u HCRu) T1 T2 (subst sigma t1) Hbody Hlcu HCRu).
```

Wait, there's an issue with `pose (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1))`: x is a local definition; using it in `destruct (Nat.eqb x a)` etc. should be fine. But `specialize (IHprem x HxL ...)`: x is a let-bound variable; OK.

Another issue: the `injection Hy as Hy'. subst T'` where Hy : Some T1 = Some T' (after update x T1 y = Some T'). With y = x, update Gamma x T1 x = if Nat.eqb x x then Some T1 else ... = Some T1. But after destruct eqb and subst y, Hy should have been rewritten by the destruct: destruct (Nat.eqb x y) in Hy rewrites the match inside Hy. So Hy : Some T1 = Some T'. injection Hy as Hy' gives T1 = T'. subst T'. ✓.

Hmm, but `destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E` when Hy contains `if Nat.eqb x y then Some T1 else Gamma y`: the destruct will substitute the if in Hy? Typically destruct with eqn rewrites occurrences in the goal and hypotheses (dependent destruction isn't needed for simple matches). Yes, `destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E` will replace the if in Hy with the branches. Then `injection Hy`. Since we're in the eqn=true branch, Hy : Some T1 = Some T' (after reduction). ✓.

Then `unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact HCRw.` Goal after destruct: CR T' (extend sigma x w y) — wait, the goal is CR T' (sigma' y) where sigma' = extend sigma x w. After subst y := x, subst T' := T1, goal CR T1 (extend sigma x w x) = CR T1 w? `unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl.` gives CR T1 w (since if true then w else ...). exact HCRw ✓.

False branch: goal CR T' (extend sigma x w y); unfold extend; rewrite E (eqb x y = false) → sigma y; apply Hcr; exact Hy. ✓.

New section:

```coq
Fixpoint CR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => sn_f t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => forall u, lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.

Definition CR_ok (T : ty) : Prop :=
  (forall t, CR T t -> sn_f t) /\
  (forall t t', CR T t -> fstep t t' -> CR T t') /\
  (forall t, neutral t -> (forall t', fstep t t' -> CR T t') -> CR T t) /\
  (forall x, CR T (tm_fvar x)).

Lemma sn_f_app_l : forall t u, sn_f (tm_app t u) -> sn_f t.
Proof.
  intros t u H. remember (tm_app t u) as s eqn:E. revert t u E.
  induction H as [s Hs IH]; intros t u E; subst s.
  apply sn_f_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  exact (IH (tm_app t' u) (F_App1 Hstep) t' u eq_refl).
Qed.

Lemma CR_ok_all : forall T, CR_ok T.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2].
  - unfold CR_ok. simpl. split.
    + intros t H; exact H.
    + split.
      * intros t t' H Hstep. destruct H as [s Hs]. apply Hs. exact Hstep.
      * split.
        -- intros t Hn Hred. apply sn_f_intro. exact Hred.
        -- intros x. apply sn_f_intro. intros t' Hstep.
           exact (fstep_fvar_inv x t' Hstep).
  - destruct IH1 as [IH1_sn [IH1_step [IH1_cand IH1_fvar]]].
    destruct IH2 as [IH2_sn [IH2_step [IH2_cand IH2_fvar]]].
    unfold CR_ok. simpl. split.
    + intros t HCR. apply sn_f_app_l with (u := tm_fvar 0).
      apply IH2_sn. apply HCR. apply lc_fvar. apply IH1_fvar.
    + split.
      * intros t t' HCR Hstep u Hlcu HCRu.
        apply IH2_step with (t := tm_app t u).
        -- apply HCR. exact Hlcu. exact HCRu.
        -- apply F_App1. exact Hstep.
      * split.
        -- intros t Hn Hred u Hlcu HCRu.
           assert (Haux : forall u, sn_f u -> lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app t u)).
           { intros u0 HSN. induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
             intros Hlc HCRs.
             apply IH2_cand.
             ++ simpl. exact I.
             ++ intros s' Hstep.
                destruct (fstep_app_inv t s s' Hstep) as
                  [[T0 [body [Heq1 Heq2]]] | [[t1' [Hst Heq]] | [s2 [Hst Heq]]]].
                ** subst t. unfold neutral in Hn. simpl in Hn. destruct Hn.
                ** subst s'. apply (Hred t1' Hst u Hlc HCRs).
                ** subst s'. apply IH.
                   --- exact Hst.
                   --- apply fstep_lc with (t := s); assumption.
                   --- apply IH1_step with (t := s); assumption.
           }
           apply Haux.
           ++ apply IH1_sn. exact HCRu.
           ++ exact Hlcu.
           ++ exact HCRu.
        -- intros x u Hlcu HCRu.
           assert (Haux : forall u, sn_f u -> lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app (tm_fvar x) u)).
           { intros u0 HSN. induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
             intros Hlc HCRs.
             apply IH2_cand.
             ++ simpl. exact I.
             ++ intros s' Hstep.
                destruct (fstep_app_inv (tm_fvar x) s s' Hstep) as
                  [[T0 [body [Heq1 Heq2]]] | [[t1' [Hst Heq]] | [s2 [Hst Heq]]]].
                ** discriminate Heq1.
                ** subst s'. exfalso. exact (fstep_fvar_inv x t1' Hst).
                ** subst s'. apply IH.
                   --- exact Hst.
                   --- apply fstep_lc with (t := s); assumption.
                   --- apply IH1_step with (t := s); assumption.
           }
           apply Haux.
           ++ apply IH1_sn. exact HCRu.
           ++ exact Hlcu.
           ++ exact HCRu.
Qed.

Lemma CR_sn : forall T t, CR T t -> sn_f t.
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [H _]. exact H. Qed.

Lemma CR_step : forall T t t', CR T t -> fstep t t' -> CR T t'.
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [_ [H _]]. exact H. Qed.

Lemma CR_cand : forall T t, neutral t ->
  (forall t', fstep t t' -> CR T t') -> CR T t.
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [_ [_ [H _]]]. exact H. Qed.

Lemma CR_fvar : forall T x, CR T (tm_fvar x).
Proof. intros T. destruct (CR_ok_all T) as [_ [_ [_ H]]]. exact H. Qed.

Lemma CR_app_abs : forall u, sn_f u -> forall T1 T2 body,
  (forall w, lc_at 0 w -> CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open body w)) ->
  lc_at 0 u -> CR T1 u -> CR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros u HSN. induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
  intros T1 T2 body Hbody Hlc HCRu.
  apply CR_cand.
  - simpl. exact I.
  - intros s' Hstep.
    destruct (fstep_app_inv (tm_abs T1 body) s s' Hstep) as
      [[T0 [body0 [Heq1 Heq2]]] | [[t1' [Hst Heq]] | [s2 [Hst Heq]]]].
    + injection Heq1 as E1 E2. subst T0. subst body0. subst s'.
      apply Hbody; assumption.
    + subst s'. exfalso. exact (fstep_abs_inv T1 body t1' Hst).
    + subst s'. apply IH.
      * exact Hst.
      * exact Hbody.
      * apply fstep_lc with (t := s); assumption.
      * apply CR_step with (t := s); assumption.
Qed.

Lemma CR_if : forall T t1 t2 t3,
  sn_f t1 -> CR T t2 -> CR T t3 -> CR T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros T t1 t2 t3 HSN. revert T t2 t3.
  induction HSN as [s Hs IH].
  intros T t2 t3 H2 H3.
  apply CR_cand.
  - simpl. exact I.
  - intros s' Hstep.
    destruct (fstep_if_inv s t2 t3 s' Hstep) as
      [[E1 E2] | [[E1 E2] | [s0 [Hst Heq]]]].
    + subst s. subst s'. exact H2.
    + subst s. subst s'. exact H3.
    + subst s'. apply IH; assumption.
Qed.

Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall a, lc_at 0 (sigma a)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> CR T (sigma x)).

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; auto.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3. reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  <{ Gamma |-- t \in T }> ->
  forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> CR T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty. induction Hty as
    [Gamma x T0 Hx
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IHprem
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma
    | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T0 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
  - (* T_Var *) intros sigma Henv. destruct Henv as [Hlc Hcr].
    simpl. apply Hcr. exact Hx.
  - (* T_Abs *) intros sigma Henv. destruct Henv as [Hlc Hcr].
    simpl. intros u Hlcu HCRu.
    pose (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
    { intro Hin.
      assert (Hin' : In x (L ++ fv t1)) by (apply in_or_app; left; exact Hin).
      pose proof (fresh_gt (L ++ fv t1) x Hin'). lia. }
    assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1)).
    { intro Hin.
      assert (Hin' : In x (L ++ fv t1)) by (apply in_or_app; right; exact Hin).
      pose proof (fresh_gt (L ++ fv t1) x Hin'). lia. }
    assert (Hbody : forall w, lc_at 0 w -> CR T1 w -> CR T2 (open (subst sigma t1) w)).
    { intros w Hlcw HCRw.
      assert (Henv : env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (extend sigma x w)).
      { split.
        - intros a. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x a) eqn:E.
          + exact Hlcw.
          + exact Hlc.
        - intros y T' Hy. unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
          + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y.
            injection Hy as Hy'. subst T'.
            unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact HCRw.
          + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. unfold extend. rewrite E.
            apply Hcr. exact Hy. }
      destruct Henv as [Henv_lc Henv_cr].
      specialize (IHprem x HxL (extend sigma x w) (conj Henv_lc Henv_cr)).
      rewrite (subst_open_rec_lc t1 0 (tm_fvar x) (extend sigma x w) Henv_lc) in IHprem.
      rewrite subst_extend_same in IHprem.
      rewrite (subst_extend_neq t1 sigma x w Hxfv) in IHprem.
      unfold open in IHprem. exact IHprem. }
    apply (CR_app_abs u (CR_sn T1 u HCRu) T1 T2 (subst sigma t1) Hbody Hlcu HCRu).
  - (* T_App *) intros sigma Henv. destruct Henv as [Hlc Hcr]. simpl.
    apply (IH1 sigma (conj Hlc Hcr)).
    + apply subst_lc with (k := 0).
      * apply typing_lc with (Gamma := Gamma) (T := T1). exact H2.
      * exact Hlc.
    + apply IH2. exact (conj Hlc Hcr).
  - (* T_True *) intros sigma Henv. simpl.
    apply sn_f_intro. intros t' Hstep. exact (fstep_true_inv t' Hstep).
  - (* T_False *) intros sigma Henv. simpl.
    apply sn_f_intro. intros t' Hstep. exact (fstep_false_inv t' Hstep).
  - (* T_If *) intros sigma Henv. destruct Henv as [Hlc Hcr]. simpl.
    apply CR_if.
    + exact (IH1 sigma (conj Hlc Hcr)).
    + apply IH2. exact (conj Hlc Hcr).
    + apply IH3. exact (conj Hlc Hcr).
Qed.
```

Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (HCR : CR T t).
  { pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) ltac:(split; [intros a; apply lc_fvar | intros x T' Hx; unfold empty in Hx; discriminate Hx])) as H.
    rewrite subst_id in H. exact H. }
  apply sn_f_sn. apply CR_sn with (T := T). exact HCR.
Qed.
```

Let me rewrite the whole lemma:

```
Lemma subst_extend_neq : forall t sigma x u,
  ~ In x (fv t) -> subst (extend sigma x u) t = subst sigma t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros sigma x u H; simpl; auto.
  - (* fvar *) unfold extend.
    destruct (Nat.eqb x a) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst a. exfalso. apply H. simpl. auto.
    + reflexivity.
  - (* app *)
    assert (H1 : ~ In x (fv t1)) by (apply notin_app_l with (L2 := fv t2); exact H).
    assert (H2 : ~ In x (fv t2)) by (apply notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t1); exact H).
    change (tm_app (subst (extend sigma x u) t1) (subst (extend sigma x u) t2) =
            tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)).
    rewrite (IHt1 sigma x u H1), (IHt2 sigma x u H2). reflexivity.
  - (* abs *)
    change (tm_abs T (subst (extend sigma x u) t) = tm_abs T (subst sigma t)).
    rewrite (IHt sigma x u H). reflexivity.
  - (* if *)
    assert (H1 : ~ In x (fv t1)) by (apply notin_app_l with (L2 := fv t2 ++ fv t3); exact H).
    assert (H2 : ~ In x (fv t2)) by (apply notin_app_l with (L2 := fv t3); apply notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t1); exact H).
    assert (H3 : ~ In x (fv t3)) by (apply notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t1 ++ fv t2); exact H).
    change (tm_if (subst (extend sigma x u) t1) (subst (extend sigma x u) t2) (subst (extend sigma x u) t3) =
            tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)).
    rewrite (IHt1 sigma x u H1), (IHt2 sigma x u H2), (IHt3 sigma x u H3). reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Hmm, the `change` might not be necessary after simpl; the goal after simpl is already in that form. But H after simpl: the initial `simpl` in the tactic line applies to the goal only, not H. The H hypothesis is `~ In x (fv t)` where t is the current subterm. For app, H : ~ In x (fv (tm_app t1 t2)), so `notin_app_l with (L2 := fv t2)` needs H to have the form ~ In x (fv t1 ++ fv t2). It doesn't unless we `simpl in H`. Let me add `simpl in H` before the assertions in app/if cases.

Also note: `rewrite` with the fully applied equality (no premises) is unconditional. ✓.

For the if case: H : ~ In x (fv (tm_if t1 t2 t3)); simpl in H gives ~ In x (fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3). Note ++ is right-associative? In Coq, `++` is right associative: `fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3` = `fv t1 ++ (fv t2 ++ fv t3)`. The fv definition is `fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3` which parses the same. For H1: ~In x (fv t1): apply notin_app_l with (L2 := fv t2 ++ fv t3) ✓. For H2: ~In x (fv t2): H gives ~In x (fv t1 ++ (fv t2 ++ fv t3)); notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t1) gives ~In x (fv t2 ++ fv t3); then notin_app_l with (L2 := fv t3) gives ~In x (fv t2) ✓. I can do it in one tactic: `apply notin_app_l with (L2 := fv t3); apply notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t1); exact H`. ✓. H3: notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t1 ++ fv t2) — but the list is fv t1 ++ (fv t2 ++ fv t3); notin_app_r with L1 := fv t1 gives ~In x (fv t2 ++ fv t3), then notin_app_r with L1 := fv t2 gives ~In x (fv t3). So: `apply notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t2); apply notin_app_r with (L1 := fv t1); exact H`. ✓.

From Stdlib Require Import Arith.PeanoNat.
From Stdlib Require Import Lists.List.
From Stdlib Require Import Lia.
Definition atom := nat.
Fixpoint sum_list (L : list atom) : nat :=
  match L with nil => 0 | x :: xs => x + sum_list xs end.
Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (sum_list L).
Lemma fresh_gt : forall L x, In x L -> x < fresh L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl in Hin. simpl. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin]; lia.
Qed.
```

Let me write:
```
Lemma sn_f_app_l : forall t u, sn_f (tm_app t u) -> sn_f t.
Proof.
  intros t u H.
  induction H using sn_f_ind with
    (P := fun s => forall a b, s = tm_app a b -> sn_f a).
  - intros s Hrec IH a b Heq. subst s.
    apply sn_f_intro. intros a' Hstep.
    apply (IH (tm_app a' b) (F_App1 Hstep) a' b eq_refl).

```
Wait, the induction principle instantiated: induction H using sn_f_ind with (P := ...). Coq will generate goals for the case and then the final goal? ```
Lemma sn_f_app_l : forall t u, sn_f (tm_app t u) -> sn_f t.

  intros t u H.
  pose proof (sn_f_ind (fun s => forall a b, s = tm_app a b -> sn_f a)

Alternative: just prove it by strong induction on the SN derivation using a helper with an explicit equation premise:

```
Lemma sn_f_app_l_aux : forall s, sn_f s ->
  forall t u, s = tm_app t u -> sn_f t.
Proof.
  intros s H. induction H as [s Hs IH].
  intros t u Heq. subst s.
  apply sn_f_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  apply (IH (tm_app t' u) (F_App1 Hstep) t' u eq_refl).
